PIN Policy Analysis
ClientPin2-PinPolicy F-2 & F-3 logic
Specification Update (W3C WebAuthn Level 4 Pull Request & Authenticate 2026 CTAP2 TWG Consensus):
Following the W3C WebAuthn Level 4 Pull Request (webauthn_level4_pull_request.html / webauthn_level4_mtc_proposal.html), the Merkle Tree Certificate attestation format has been consolidated into a single compact format identifier with an opt-in diagnostic extension:
- fmt: "mtc" (consolidating mtc-mds into mtc): The single, compact Merkle Tree Certificate attestation statement format where attStmt carries only { alg, sig, mtcCert } with a 19-byte 2-field mtcKeyRef ({ caId, serial }) (strictly omitting both the 1,322 B inline pubKey and 147 B inclusion proof, saving 1,478 bytes on the wire for ML-DSA-44 batch/enterprise keys) and the verifier resolves the key via mtcTrustAnchor.batchKeys in FIDO MDS3 or an Enterprise Attestation Manifest.
- "mtc-debug" Registration Extension: An opt-in CTAP 2.3 / WebAuthn Level 4 registration extension (authenticatorGetInfo.extensions includes "mtc-debug") that returns { pubKey, notBefore, notAfter, firmwareVersion, mtcProof, rootHash, deviceSerial? } inside authenticatorData.extensions["mtc-debug"] for standalone conformance and diagnostic verification without pre-loaded batchKeys, enforcing a strict privacy gate where deviceSerial is emitted only when epAtt: true.

Fidont authenticator simulator platform performance numbers are for relative comparison as implemented with amateur optimization by me:)

Attribution & Scope of Fidont:
Fidont is an authenticator simulator designed for prototyping new specifications and testing authenticator conformance tools. It is based on the Google OpenSK Rust codebase. However, Fidont is not suitable for production security use outside of certification, conformance testing, and prototyping new features.

Executive Summary of Run 39 (fido_conformance_debug.log L1–L37805 & opensk_rtt.log L82262–L91382, 260 Passed / 30 Skipped / 0 Failed / 0 Lockups in 13m 46.44s):
1. 100% PASS (260 / 260) on [P2] WebAuthn Level 4 Credential Public Key Policy Enforcement Across All Registration Entry Points (js/mtc-validation.js, js/meta.js, & Authr-MakeCred-Resp-1.js):
- Pre-Dispatch & Direct Entry-Point Enforcement (mtc-validation.js:2402-2452, mtc-validation.js:3650-3660, mtc-validation.js:4310-4325, mtc-validation.js:4500-4525): Enforces 'credential-cose' (webauthn/index.bs:4354-4363) once on parsed authDataStruct.coseKeyBytes inside verifyAttestationByFmt before format dispatch ("none", "mtc", "packed"), directly inside verifyMtc and verifyPacked (x5c + self-attestation), and inside Authr-MakeCred-Resp-1.js (P-02 & P-06), while preserving generic 'cose' / 'checkpoint' (RFC 9053 §2.1) for batch/attestation/checkpoint keys.
- P-02 & P-06 (100% PASS, 0 [ASSERTION FAILED] events): With 'Ed25519': 6 and 'Ed448': 7 added to COSE_CRV in meta.js:1046-1047 and Authr-MakeCred-Resp-1.js:210-214, both P-02 (18 algorithms verified via validateCredentialPublicKeyCose) and P-06 (54 live hardware USB HID registrations + 58 real, re-signed credential-policy registrations in runP06CredentialPolicyRegistrationMatrixSelfTest across "none", "mtc", and "packed" x5c/self) passed 100% on Attempt 1!
2. Simultaneous Host USB Round-Trip (fido_conformance_debug.log) & Hardware MCU Execution (opensk_rtt.log) Attestation Benchmark with ML-DSA-65 (alg: -49, NIST Level 3) Batch Key (159 Host / 160 MCU MakeCredential Exchanges):
- All 11 PQ + Composite Algorithms — "packed" (12,469.3 B / 211.8f avg, 10,335 B attStmt with 7,004 B ML-DSA-65 X.509 cert): Mean Host USB = 2,099.7 ms (median 1,867.7 ms, sum 23.10 s), Mean MCU = 1,225.7 ms (median 1,008.2 ms, sum 13.48 s) $\implies$ 874.0 ms (4.13 ms/frame) of pure USB HID wire transfer overhead per registration (9.62 s pure USB transfer across 11 PQ algorithms)!

0.0000000000000000 Run 37 (02:33:31 – 03:23:28 PDT, 2026-10-30, 14m 03.04s Active Execution) Full-Suite Conformance (260 / 260 100.00% PASS): [P2] Generic EdDSA (Ed25519 vs. Ed448) Provider Parity & Algorithm Context Separation + Simultaneous Host USB & MCU RTT Attestation Timing Analysis (ML-DSA-65 Batch Key)

Executive Summary of Run 37 (fido_conformance_debug.log L1–L38147 & opensk_rtt.log L63843–L73102, 260 Passed / 30 Skipped / 0 Failed / 0 Lockups in 14m 03.04s active execution / 49m 56.29s wall-clock):
1. [P2] Generic EdDSA (Ed25519 vs. Ed448) Provider Parity & Algorithm Context Separation (js/mtc-validation.js & dependencies/cryptodep.js):
- Generic EdDSA ('EdDSA' / COSE -8) Subtype Resolution (mtc-validation.js:1654-1823, dependencies/cryptodep.js:938-1150): Generic 'EdDSA' / -8 resolves its required public key byte length (32 B vs. 57 B) and raw signature byte length (64 B vs. 114 B) from the actual supported key subtype (Ed25519 / crv: 6 vs. Ed448 / crv: 7), while explicit 'Ed448' / COSE -53 remains strictly restricted to Ed448 (crv: 7, 57 B key, 114 B sig). Added extractEd448PubKeyFromPem (OID 1.3.101.113 = 06032b6571) and routed Ed448 X.509 verification through verifyRawEd448 (@noble/curves/ed448) so standalone OpenSSL and packaged Electron 41.7.0 BoringSSL verify Ed448 certificates identically.
- Algorithm Context Separation (RFC 7518 §3.4 JOSE vs. WebAuthn §5.8.5 Credential Keys vs. RFC 9053 §2.1 Generic COSE Attestation / Checkpoint, mtc-validation.js:1888-2218 & dependencies/cryptodep.js:761-828):
- JOSE (context: 'jws' & string 'structured-envelope', RFC 7518 §3.4): ES256, ES384, ES512, and ES256K strictly bind their single curve (P-256, P-384, P-521, secp256k1) and fixed-width ieee-p1363 encoding (64, 96, 132, 64 bytes).
- WebAuthn Credential Public Keys (context: 'credential-cose', WebAuthn §5.8.5): Enforces -7/-9 → crv: 1 (P-256), -35/-51 → crv: 2 (P-384), -36/-52 → crv: 3 (P-521), and -8/-19 → crv: 6 (Ed25519) on credential public keys. (Note: During Run 37 itself, 'credential-cose' was only wired into verifyPacked self-attestation and helper unit tests; subsequent post-Run-37 integration wired validateCredentialPublicKeyCose pre-dispatch across verifyAttestationByFmt for "none", "mtc", and "packed", direct verifyMtc, direct verifyPacked (x5c + self), and Authr-MakeCred-Resp-1.js (P-02 & P-06).)
- Generic COSE Attestation / Checkpoint (context: 'cose' / 'checkpoint' / 'x509' / integer 'structured-envelope', RFC 9053 §2.1): Binds kty: 2 (EC2) and the SHA digest (-7 → SHA-256, -35 → SHA-384, -36 → SHA-512) while validating the key's actual supported curve (crv: 1, 2, 3 plus crv: 8 for -7), coordinate byte lengths (32, 48, 66), on-curve point geometry, and signature length (ieee-p1363 2 * coordLen or strict ASN.1 DER).
2. Simultaneous Host USB Round-Trip (fido_conformance_debug.log) & Hardware MCU Execution (opensk_rtt.log) Attestation Benchmark with ML-DSA-65 (alg: -49, NIST Level 3) Batch Key (159 Host / 160 MCU MakeCredential Exchanges):
- All 11 PQ + Composite Algorithms — "packed" (12,469.3 B / 211.8f avg, 10,335 B attStmt with 7,004 B ML-DSA-65 X.509 cert): Mean Host USB = 2,634.3 ms (median 2,378.8 ms, sum 28.98 s), Mean MCU = 1,761.0 ms (median 1,465.9 ms, sum 19.37 s) $\implies$ 873.3 ms (4.12 ms/frame) of pure USB HID wire transfer overhead per registration!
- All 11 PQ + Composite Algorithms — "mtc" (5,490.3 B / 93.5f avg, 3,351 B attStmt with 19 B mtcKeyRef): Mean Host USB = 1,454.5 ms (P-06) / 1,843.5 ms (before()), Mean MCU = 1,056.8 ms (P-06) / 1,445.4 ms (before()) $\implies$ 397.7–398.1 ms (4.25–4.26 ms/frame) of USB HID wire transfer overhead, saving 1,179.8 ms (-44.8%) in P-06 and 790.8 ms (-30.0%) in before() total Host USB round-trip time, 475.2–475.6 ms (-54.5%) of pure USB wire transfer time, and 6,979 B (118.3 frames) on every post-quantum registration!

Executive Summary of Run 36 (fido_conformance_debug.log L1–L37797 & opensk_rtt.log L54689–L63840, 260 Passed / 30 Skipped / 0 Failed / 0 Lockups in 13m 45.68s):
1. [P1/P2] Imported alg Descriptor Boundary & Private Brand Enforcement (js/mtc-validation.js & dependencies/cryptodep.js):
- Closed the descriptor-injection boundary where resolveSignatureAlgorithmSpec previously returned any imported object with string keyType and id:
- Private Descriptor Brand (mtc-validation.js:349-370): Added closure-private ALG_SPEC_BRAND = Symbol('MtcValidatedAlgorithmSpec') and VALIDATED_ALG_SPECS = new WeakSet(). Only descriptors constructed internally by resolveSignatureAlgorithmSpec carry this brand; verifyEnvelopeSignatureAgainstTrustedRoots, bridgeProvider.verifyCertSignature, and fallbackProvider.verifyCertSignature reject any unbranded object immediately.
- Strict JWS & Structured Raw alg Schema (mtc-validation.js:1733-1806, mtc-validation.js:2235-2388): Enforced exact, unpadded RFC 7515 §4.1.1 StringOrURI strings at JWS boundaries (jws-compact, jws-descriptor, structured-protected), rejecting imported descriptor objects (ES256 with sigEncoding: 'der', PS256 selecting EC + DER), numeric JWS alg: -7, and whitespace-padded ' ES256 '. In structured-raw, permitted only exact registered strings or COSE safe integers (rejecting unregistered descriptor objects and numeric strings like '-7').
- Canonical Policy Verification in Production Bridge (dependencies/cryptodep.js:899-959): Hardened verifySignature to look up BUILTIN_ALG_SPECS[HashAlgOrSpec.id] and verify keyType, nodeDigest, rsaPadding, pssSaltLength, p1363SigLength, and context-bound sigEncoding before accepting any descriptor object.

Executive Summary of Run 35 (fido_conformance_debug.log L1–L39170 & opensk_rtt.log L45564–L54688, 260 Passed / 30 Skipped / 0 Failed / 0 Lockups in 14m 06.34s):
1. [P1/P2] Validated Algorithm Descriptor Contract (SIGNATURE_ALGORITHM_SPECS), Wire-Encoding Separation (ieee-p1363 vs. der), COSE_Key Family/Curve Binding & RFC 7515 §4.1.11 crit Header Enforcement:
- Replaced digest-only resolveEnvelopeHashAlg with resolveSignatureAlgorithmSpec(alg, context) and SIGNATURE_ALGORITHM_SPECS, passing a validated algSpec ({ jwsAlg, coseAlg, family, kty, crv, namedCurve, hashAlg, rsaPadding, saltLength, minModulusBits, jwsSignatureEncoding, x509SignatureEncoding, wireEncoding }) into verifySignature and verifySignatureCOSE:
- RSA Padding & Salt Length (PS256 vs. RS256, RFC 7518 §3.5): Standards-valid PS256 (2048-bit RSA, RSA_PKCS1_PSS_PADDING, saltLength: 32) is now ACCEPTED across jws-compact, jws-descriptor, structured-protected, and structured-raw, while PS256-labelled RSA PKCS#1 v1.5 signatures and wrong-salt PSS (saltLength: 16) are REJECTED.
- Cross-Family & Cross-Curve Key Confusion: RS256-labelled EC signatures (jws-compact, structured-protected, and subtreeCheckpoint), ES256-labelled RSA signatures, and ES256-labelled OKP/Ed25519 COSE_Key batch/device keys are REJECTED (assertCoseKeyMatchesAlgorithmSpec).
- Wire Encoding (ieee-p1363 vs. der, RFC 7518 §3.4): JWS ECDSA (context: 'jws') strictly requires fixed-width R || S (64 B for ES256/ES256K, 96 B for ES384, 132 B for ES512) and rejects ASN.1 DER Ecdsa-Sig-Value, while CTAP2/X.509 (context: 'x509' / 'cose') preserves DER verification.
- Critical Header Enforcement (RFC 7515 §4.1.11): validateJwsCriticalHeaders rejects unknown crit extensions (crit: ["exp"]), empty/non-array crit, duplicate entries, standard headers in crit, and missing header parameters.
2. Simultaneous Host USB Round-Trip (fido_conformance_debug.log) & Hardware MCU Execution (opensk_rtt.log) Attestation Benchmark (159 Host / 160 MCU MakeCredential Exchanges):

Executive Summary of Run 33 (fido_conformance_debug.log L1–L39162 & opensk_rtt.log L27316–L36440, 260 Passed / 30 Skipped / 0 Failed / 0 Lockups in 14m 09.70s):
1. Resolution of All 4 Run 32 ResidentKey.js Errors (P-2, P-3, P-5, P-6 → 100% Attempt-1 PASS):
- In Run 32 (17:35:04–17:50:04 PDT), ResidentKey.js P-2 and P-3 failed on authenticatorGetNextAssertion(0x08) with [GetNextAssertion] Response authData.flags.up (false) MUST match requested UP (undefined)!, and their 4 Mocha retries leaked 20 discoverable credentials (4 × 3 + 4 × 2 = 20), causing P-5 and P-6 to fail with CTAP2_ERR_KEY_STORE_FULL(40).
- Root-cause analysis identified that app/js/sandbox.js:20 used return target[key] || target.window[key] inside its with (sandboxProxy) get trap, causing boolean false stored in lastGetAssertionExpectedUp on the sandbox target scope to short-circuit to target.window.lastGetAssertionExpectedUp (undefined).
- Fixing app/js/sandbox.js:20 to (key in target) ? target[key] : target.window[key] and synchronizing window.lastGetAssertionExpectedUp in CTAP2.js:1188-1244 resolved all 4 errors: P-1, P-2, P-3, P-5, and P-6 passed on their first attempt with zero retries!
2. P1 Signed-Envelope Cryptographic Binding & Independent Signer Root Pinning (js/mtc-validation.js):
- Verified in both standalone unit tests (ALL_MTC_VALIDATION_UNIT_TESTS_PASSED across positive & negative vectors in js/mtc-envelope-vectors.js and js/test-mtc-envelope-binding.js) and the full 38-suite conformance run (260 / 260 PASS):
- Removed caller-controlled signingInputBytes from imported structured envelopes (raw-cose-sign1-envelope, cose-sign1, jws, raw), reconstructing signing input internally over exact parsed payload bytes.
- Enforced independent trustedSignerRoots configuration (options.trustedSignerRoots / metadata.attestationRootCertificates), ignoring signedEnvelope.rootCertificates and treating x5c strictly as a candidate chain.
- Enforced atomic all-or-nothing import before branding any row with Symbol('MtcAuthenticatedBootstrap').
3. Simultaneous Host USB Round-Trip (fido_conformance_debug.log) & Hardware MCU Execution (opensk_rtt.log) Attestation Timing Dissection:
- Because Run 33 captured both fido_conformance_debug.log (Host USB ms) and opensk_rtt.log (MCU ms) simultaneously across all 159 valid MakeCredential exchanges (54 in P-06, 18 in before(), 10 in mtcDebug.js + EntepriseAttestation.js), we can directly separate MCU cryptographic execution time from physical USB Full-Speed HID transport overhead (Host USB ms - MCU ms):

0.000000000000 Run 31 (16:27:43 – 16:43:52 PDT, 2026-10-29, 16m 09.10s) Full-Suite Conformance (260 / 260 100.00% PASS): ML-DSA-87 (alg: -50) Batch Attestation Benchmark & Comprehensive Comparison Across All Batch Key Sizes (ES256 P-256 vs. ML-DSA-44 vs. ML-DSA-65 vs. ML-DSA-87)

Executive Summary of Run 31 (opensk_rtt.log L6862–L15997, 260 Passed / 31 Skipped / 0 Failed / 0 Lockups in 16m 09.10s):
1. Complete 4-Tier Batch Attestation Key Comparison (ES256 P-256 vs. ML-DSA-44 Run 29 vs. ML-DSA-65 Run 30 vs. ML-DSA-87 Run 31):
- With Run 31 configured to use ML-DSA-87 (alg: -50, NIST Security Level 5, serial: 4098 / 0x0007000000000002) as the authenticator's post-quantum batch attestation key (2,602 B COSE public key, 4,627 B signature, 7,644 B DER X.509v3 leaf certificate signed by the ML-DSA-87 Root CA), we now have full 38-suite conformance benchmarks (159 MakeCredential ceremonies per run) across all three NIST FIPS 204 ML-DSA security levels (ML-DSA-44 in Run 29, ML-DSA-65 in Run 30, ML-DSA-87 in Run 31) alongside the classical ES256 (alg: -7, P-256) batch key:
- fmt: "packed" Post-Quantum X.509 attStmt Scales from 8,806 B (ML-DSA-44) → 10,335 B (ML-DSA-65) → 12,293 B (ML-DSA-87): Because "packed" must carry both the batch key's signature (2,420 B / 3,309 B / 4,627 B) and its X.509v3 leaf certificate (6,364 B / 7,004 B / 7,644 B, which embeds the batch public key plus the 4,627 B ML-DSA-87 Root CA signature), a single MakeCredential "packed" response reaches 13,511–15,068 B (230–256 USB HID frames) with an ML-DSA-87 batch key — consuming up to 92.0% of OpenSK's 16,384 B maxMsgSize buffer!
- fmt: "mtc" Eliminates 6,339 B (ML-DSA-44), 6,979 B (ML-DSA-65), and 7,619 B (ML-DSA-87) on Every Registration: Because fmt: "mtc" replaces the entire X.509 certificate (6,364 B / 7,004 B / 7,644 B) with a constant 19 B 2-field mtcKeyRef ({ caId, serial }, 24 B CBOR mtcCert), the wire savings of "mtc" over "packed" grow as the batch key size increases:
- ML-DSA-44 Batch Key (Run 29): attStmt drops from 8,806 B to 2,462 B (-6,344 B / -72.0%); total PQ BCNT drops from 10,024–11,581 B (170–197f) to 3,685–5,242 B (63–89f, saving 6,339 B / 107.4 frames).
- ML-DSA-65 Batch Key (Run 30): attStmt drops from 10,335 B to 3,351 B (-6,984 B / -67.6%); total PQ BCNT drops from 11,553–13,110 B (196–223f) to 4,574–6,131 B (78–104f, saving 6,979 B / 118.3 frames).
- ML-DSA-87 Batch Key (Run 31): attStmt drops from 12,293 B to 4,669 B (-7,624 B / -62.0%); total PQ BCNT drops from 13,511–15,068 B (230–256f) to 5,892–7,449 B (100–127f, saving 7,619 B / 129.3 frames).
2. Zero Lockups Across ThirdPartyPayment & LargeBlobs (opensk_rtt.log L15254–L15997):
- Verified both root-cause fixes from the lockup analysis (fido_ctap23_lockup_analysis_report.md):
- ThirdPartyPayment P-2 (L15460–L15503): Sent options: { up: false } in GetAssertion, allowing OpenSK to verify thirdPartyPayment immediately in 1,004.7 ms (398 B response) without blocking on an unprompted physical button press.
- Authr-LargeBlobs-1 (L15955–L15997): Enforced const maxFragmentLength = (getInfo.maxMsgSize || 1024) - 64; (984 B on 1,048 B write instead of 16,320 B), completing P-1..P-4 and F-1..F-4 in 2.5s with 0 ERR_EXCEEDED_MAX_LENGTH (0x39) errors.
3. Default Optional UP UX Checkbox (default_optional_up = false) & Universal UP Response Bit Validation:
- Added a new UI configuration checkbox in app/js/view.js:177-189 ("Default Optional UP in GetAssertion (up=true when omitted)", defaulting to unchecked / false) wired through preload.js:196-203 and CTAP2.js:563-618 (resolveOptionalGetAssertionOptions).
- Added universal validateResponseAuthDataUpFlag(commandBuffer, responseBuffer) in CTAP2.js:1197-1244 (plus explicit assertions in Authr-GetAssertion-Req-2.js, Authr-GetAssertion-Resp-1.js, and ResidentKey.js) so every successful MakeCredential and GetAssertion response is checked to ensure authData.flags.up matches the effective requested UP (true for MakeCredential and GetAssertion when up !== false; false when up === false).

Timing Metrics Note (Host USB Round-Trip vs. MCU Execution + USB Transfer):

Executive Summary of Run 30 (fido_conformance_debug.log L1–L37907, 260 Passed / 31 Skipped / 0 Failed in 13m 41.92s):
1. One Strict, Reusable MTC & Common Attestation Verification Pipeline (js/mtc-validation.js):
- Consolidated all attestation verification across Authr-MakeCred-Resp-1.js, CTAP2/Protocol/Extensions/mtcDebug.js, and CTAP2/Protocol/Options/EntepriseAttestation.js into a single module (modules/ctap2.3-conformance-module/js/mtc-validation.js, loaded once via manifesto.json):
- Step 1 (decodeCborLossless & parseAuthDataLossless): Decodes raw CBOR bytes (response.cborBuffer preserved by CTAP2.js:1046-1052) without IEEE-754 Number precision loss, preserving 64-bit uint64 integers (serial, notBefore, notAfter, firmwareVersion) as exact JavaScript BigInt values.
- Step 2 (Exact Statement & Reference Schema): Requires fmt === "mtc", attStmt keys strictly { alg, sig, mtcCert }, and mtcCert strictly { caId, serial } (19 B mtcKeyRef: caId: 11 B, serial: 8 B uint64). Rejects any extra wire fields on mtcCert (notBefore, notAfter, mtcProof, firmwareVersion, pubKey) so wire references can never self-assert metadata.
- Step 3 (bootstrapMtcTrustState & Unforgeable Symbol('MtcAuthenticatedBootstrap')): Requires trustedEntries (and optional revocationState, authorizedInventory) to carry an unforgeable private Symbol('MtcAuthenticatedBootstrap') produced only by bootstrapMtcTrustState(), which cryptographically verifies the FIDO-MTC-SUBTREE-CHECKPOINT-V1 signature over every subtree checkpoint against metadata.attestationRootCertificates before branding the table. Resolves the authenticated entry strictly by (caId, serial).
- Step 4 (Strict mtc-debug Consistency & No Silent Proof Fallback): Validates optional authData.extensions["mtc-debug"] against the resolved authenticated entry (pubKey, notBefore, notAfter, firmwareVersion, rootHash). Treats any malformed supplied mtcProof as an immediate verification error rather than falling back to the proof-free branch.
- Step 5 (COSE Algorithm Binding & Cryptographic Signature Verification): Binds attStmt.alg to the resolved attestation COSE_Key.alg (kty, crv, key-size invariants) and verifies attStmt.sig over authData || clientDataHash (ML-DSA-44/65/87, FN-DSA-512/1024, SLH-DSA, PQ/T composites, ECDSA, EdDSA, RSA).
- Step 6 (Validity Window & Revocation Enforcement): Enforces notBefore <= nowSeconds <= notAfter (using BigInt seconds) and checks revocationState (revokedSerials, revokedCaIds, minAllowedFirmwareVersion).
- Step 7 (RFC 9162 Inclusion Proof & Checkpoint Association): When an inclusion proof is supplied (via mtc-debug or manifest metadata), hashes the canonical leaf (0x00 || leafBytes), walks inclusionPath (0x01 || left || right) within [startIndex, endIndex), and verifies the derived root equals the authenticated checkpoint rootHash.
- Step 8 (Enterprise Grant & Authorized Inventory Policy): Enforces enterpriseGranted (epAtt === true allowed only when requested for an allowlisted RP; deviceSerial in mtc-debug allowed and required only when epAtt === true) and authorizedInventory serial/deviceSerial policy.
- Format Dispatch (verifyAttestationByFmt & verifyPacked): Dispatches "none", "packed" (complete X.509v3 leaf checks: version v3, empty Subject DN only when epAtt is true or FIDO id-fido-gen-ce-aaguid matches, BasicConstraints CA:FALSE, validity window, trust chain to metadata.attestationRootCertificates, SPKI / attStmt.alg binding, and signature verification), and "mtc", while rejecting unimplemented formats with unsupported_or_unvalidated.
2. Key Findings from Run 30 Cross-Format Attestation Wire Size & Timing Analysis (ML-DSA-65 Batch Key vs. ML-DSA-44 Enterprise Key Across 159 MakeCredential Ceremonies):
- Post-Quantum "packed" with ML-DSA-65 (alg: -49) Batch Certificate Reaches 11,553–13,110 B (196–223 USB HID Frames): In Run 30, the authenticator's batch attestation key is ML-DSA-65 (alg: -49) (sig: 3,309 B, x5c[0]: 7,004 B DER X.509v3 certificate signed by the ML-DSA-87 Root CA → attStmt = 10,335 B). Across the 11 post-quantum and composite algorithms, fmt: "packed" transmits 11,553–13,110 B (196–223 USB HID frames) per registration and takes a mean of 2,701.7 ms (median: 2,332.3 ms, range: 1,548.1–4,532.5 ms).
- Post-Quantum fmt: "mtc" with ML-DSA-65 (alg: -49) Batch Key Eliminates 6,979 B (118–119 USB HID Frames) and Saves 1,151.8 ms (-42.6%) per Registration: By replacing the 7,004 B ML-DSA-65 X.509 certificate with the 19 B 2-field mtcKeyRef ({ caId, serial }, 24 B CBOR mtcCert), fmt: "mtc" reduces attStmt from 10,335 B to 3,351 B (-67.6%, -6,984 B), cutting total MakeCredential wire size (BCNT) across all 11 PQ/composite algorithms by 6,979 B (-53.2% to -60.4%, 4,574–6,131 B / 78–104 frames vs. 11,553–13,110 B / 196–223 frames) and reducing mean registration latency from 2,701.7 ms to 1,549.9 ms (-1,151.8 ms / -42.6% faster)!
- Comparison of Batch (ML-DSA-65, alg: -49) vs. Enterprise (ML-DSA-44, alg: -48) "mtc" + "mtc-debug":
- Batch "mtc" + "mtc-debug" (mtcDebug.js P-2 & F-3, serial: 4097, ML-DSA-65): authData = 2,578 B (373 B base + 2,205 B 6-field mtc-debug carrying the 1,962 B ML-DSA-65 COSE_Key), attStmt = 3,351 B (3,309 B ML-DSA-65 signature + 24 B mtcCert), total BCNT = 5,949 B (101 frames, 1,812.1–2,735.4 ms) — still 5,604–7,161 B (95–122 frames) smaller than PQ "packed" (11,553–13,110 B)!
- Enterprise "mtc" + "mtc-debug" (mtcDebug.js P-3 & EntepriseAttestation.js P-2/P-3, serial: 8192, ML-DSA-44): authData = 1,964 B (373 B base + 1,591 B 7-field mtc-debug carrying the 1,322 B ML-DSA-44 COSE_Key + deviceSerial: "123456"), attStmt = 2,462 B (2,420 B ML-DSA-44 signature + 24 B mtcCert), total BCNT = 4,448 B (76 frames, 1,675.2–2,274.8 ms)!

Executive Summary of Run 29 (fido_conformance_debug.log L1–L38383, 259 Passed / 31 Skipped / 0 Failed in 13m 19.96s):
1. Real-Time Sub-Task Progress Bars for Multi-Algorithm Sweeps:
- Added a dedicated sub-task progress indicator (.algo-sweep-progress in app/index.html:282-304, app/css/loader.css:19-76, and app/js/controller.js:189-256) that renders live step progress (k / N, percentage, format, and human-friendly COSE algorithm name via window.formatCoseAlgLabel) across all three multi-algorithm sweeps:
- getMakeCredentialResponseForAllAlgorithms() (CTAP2.js:1009-1060, 18 steps in Authr-MakeCred-Resp-1.js before(), L3820–L6418).

2. Clean Separation of Baseline Authr-MakeCred-Resp-1.js from Diagnostic mtc-debug:
- Removed the duplicate mtc-debug P-05 test and mtcDebugExt fallback from Authr-MakeCred-Resp-1.js so Authr-MakeCred-Resp-1.js (P-01..P-06, F-01) verifies "packed" and "mtc" registrations strictly against metadata.attestationRootCertificates and metadata.mtcTrustAnchors, while all 7 "mtc-debug" extension tests (P-1..P-3, F-1..F-4) run in the dedicated CTAP2/Protocol/Extensions/mtcDebug.js suite (L30773–L31700).
3. Key Findings from Cross-Format Attestation Wire Size & Timing Analysis (159 MakeCredential Ceremonies):
- Why Post-Quantum "packed" Explodes to 10,024–11,581 B (170–197 USB HID Frames): For the 7 classical algorithms (ES256, Ed25519, ES384, ES512, ES256K, Ed448, RS256), fmt: "packed" signs with the classical P-256 (alg: -7) batch key (sig: 70–72 B, x5c[0]: 600 B DER cert → attStmt = 690–692 B, total BCNT = 1,044–1,275 B / 18–22 frames). However, for the 11 post-quantum and hybrid composite algorithms (ML-DSA-44/65/87, FN-DSA-512/1024, and the 6 ML-DSA composites), fmt: "packed" must use the post-quantum ML-DSA-44 (alg: -48) X.509 batch certificate (sig: 2,420 B, x5c[0]: 6,364 B DER cert → attStmt = 8,806 B), pushing total MakeCredential wire size (BCNT) to 10,024–11,581 B (170–197 USB HID frames)!
- How fmt: "mtc" Eliminates 6,339 B (107–108 USB HID Frames) per Post-Quantum Registration: By replacing the 6,364 B X.509 certificate with a 19 B 2-field mtcKeyRef ({ caId, serial }, 24 B CBOR mtcCert map) while keeping the same 2,420 B ML-DSA-44 (alg: -48) batch signature, fmt: "mtc" holds attStmt to a constant 2,462 B across all 18 algorithms — a -72.0% (-6,344 B) reduction in attStmt size and a -54.7% to -63.2% (-6,339 B / -107.4 frames) reduction in total MakeCredential wire size (BCNT) across all 11 post-quantum/composite algorithms, cutting mean registration latency by 571.0 ms (-27.9%, from 2,045.7 ms to 1,474.7 ms)!
- Even "mtc" + "mtc-debug" (4,420–4,448 B) Is -57.5% Smaller Than PQ "packed" (10,439 B): Adding the opt-in "mtc-debug" extension (1,565 B non-enterprise / 1,591 B enterprise in authData.extensions) brings ES256 + "mtc" + "mtc-debug" to 4,420–4,448 B (75–76 frames) — still 5,991–6,019 B (101–102 frames) smaller than a single ML-DSA-44 "packed" X.509 registration (10,439 B / 177 frames).

0.0000000000.5 Key Architectural Takeaways from Cross-Format Attestation Benchmarking

  • fmt: "none" Isolates Pure Credential Key Generation Cost (187.0 ms – 1,783.0 ms):
  • Because fmt: "none" performs zero attestation signing (attStmt = {}, 1 B), its timing directly measures credential key generation + authData assembly + USB transfer:
  • Fastest algorithms (< 400 ms in "none"): FN-DSA-512 (187.0 ms, 1,217 B), FN-DSA-1024 (242.7 ms, 2,113 B), RS256 (258.6 ms, 582 B, pre-generated key), Ed25519 (282.4 ms, 352 B), ML-DSA-44 (309.7 ms, 1,632 B), ES256K (312.5 ms, 388 B), MLDSA44-Ed25519 (358.8 ms, 1,664 B), and ML-DSA-65 (391.7 ms, 2,272 B).
  • Heaviest credential keygen (> 1,400 ms in "none"): Software big-integer ECDSA scalar multiplication on P-384 (ES384: 1,430.0 ms, MLDSA87-ES384: 1,783.0 ms) and P-521 (ES512: 1,438.2 ms) dominates registration latency much more than lattice key generation (ML-DSA-44/65/87 take only 309.7–594.7 ms).
  • Post-Quantum fmt: "mtc" Saves 6,339 B (107–108 Frames) and 571.0 ms per Registration vs. Post-Quantum fmt: "packed":
  • When registering any of the 11 post-quantum or composite algorithms, both "mtc" and "packed" sign authData || clientDataHash with the same ML-DSA-44 (alg: -48, 2,420 B signature) attestation key.
  • However, "packed" must transmit the 6,364 B X.509v3 ML-DSA-44 leaf certificate (attStmt = 8,806 B, 170–197 USB HID frames), whereas "mtc" transmits only the 19 B mtcKeyRef (attStmt = 2,462 B, 63–89 USB HID frames).
  • Eliminating those 107–108 USB HID IN frames (6,339 B) reduces mean registration latency across the 11 PQ/composite algorithms from 2,045.7 ms ("packed") to 1,474.7 ms ("mtc" in P-06) and 1,405.3 ms ("mtc" in before()) — a 571.0–640.4 ms (27.9%–31.3%) speedup on every registration!
  • Why Fidont's Default Hybrid Policy ("packed" for Classical, "mtc" for Post-Quantum) Is Optimal:
  • For the 7 classical algorithms, the classical P-256 (ES256, alg: -7) batch X.509 certificate (600 B x5c + 70–72 B signature = 690–692 B attStmt, 1,044–1,275 B BCNT, 18–22 frames) is 1,776 B (30 frames) smaller than "mtc" (2,462 B attStmt with 2,420 B ML-DSA-44 signature).
  • For the 11 post-quantum & composite algorithms, "mtc" (2,462 B attStmt, 3,685–5,242 B BCNT, 63–89 frames) is 6,339 B (107–108 frames) smaller than PQ "packed" (8,806 B attStmt, 10,024–11,581 B BCNT, 170–197 frames).
  • Combining classical "packed" (7 algs) + post-quantum "mtc" (11 algs) yields a total 18-algorithm sweep payload of 58,422 B (999 frames, 24.77 s) vs. 128,150 B (2,181 frames, 32.00 s) for all-"packed" — cutting total wire traffic across the 18-algorithm sweep by 69,728 B (-54.4%, -1,182 USB HID frames)!

0.00000.3 Byte-Level CBOR & X.509 Dissection of Key Protocol Exchanges

1. Enabling Enterprise Attestation via authenticatorConfig (0x0D) (CID=0000000d, Log L185–L199, RTT L1838–L1863)

  • Request (41 B payload, 1 CTAPHID frame):
0d                                                                 # authenticatorConfig (0x0D)

3. Negative Option Validation (F-4 on CID=0000001e & F-5 on CID=00000021)

  • F-4 (enterpriseAttestation = true / 0x0a 0xf5):
  • Rejected immediately by Fidont's CBOR request decoder because enterprise_attestation expects an unsigned integer (u8), returning 1 B status 0x11 (CTAP2_ERR_CBOR_UNEXPECTED_TYPE) in < 1 ms (RTT L9418).
  • F-5 (enterpriseAttestation = 117 / 0x0a 0x18 0x75):
  • Decodes as Some(117), verifies pinUvAuthParam, and is rejected by the CTAP 2.3 option validator (enterprise_attestation != 1 && enterprise_attestation != 2), returning 1 B status 0x2C (CTAP2_ERR_INVALID_OPTION) in 196.93 ms (RTT L9885–L9905).

0.0000 Run 22 (11:51:49 – 12:11:56 PDT) Deep Dive: W3C WebAuthn Level 4 Pull Request Consolidated fmt: "mtc" (4,736–5,397 B, Saving 1,331 B Wire Payload), "mtc-debug" Extension & opensk_rtt.log Hardware Telemetry

W3C WebAuthn Level 4 PR Verification — Consolidated fmt: "mtc" (1,331 B Smaller Wire Payloads) & Opt-In "mtc-debug" Extension:
In Run 22 (2026-10-23, 11:51:49.597 – 12:11:56.384 PDT, 20m 06.79s active test execution), we updated both Fidont / OpenSK (mtc.rs, data_formats.rs, mod.rs, configure.py, and fido-mds3.json) and the FIDO CTAP 2.3 Conformance Testing Tool (Authr-MakeCred-Resp-1.js, EntepriseAttestation.js, Metadata-Stmt-1.js) to implement the W3C WebAuthn Level 4 Pull Request (webauthn_level4_pull_request.html):
- Consolidated fmt: "mtc" Omitting Inline pubKey (1,331 B Wire Reduction): By consolidating mtc-mds into fmt: "mtc" and omitting the inline 1,322 B ML-DSA-44 COSE_Key (pubKey) from attStmt (resolving it instead from metadata.mtcTrustAnchors[].batchKeys by serial), every 2-step PQ and composite MakeCredential response shrank by exactly 1,331 bytes (21 fewer 64-byte CTAPHID IN frames) compared to Run 21:
- FN-DSA-1024 (-61): 6,067 B → 4,736 B (6,778.1 ms → 5,032.7 ms, 1.75s faster over USB HID)
- ML-DSA-65 (-49): 6,226 B → 4,895 B (7,387.2 ms → 6,541.1 ms, 0.85s faster)
- MLDSA65-Ed25519 (-58): 6,258 B → 4,927 B (6,775.7 ms → 6,072.3 ms, 0.70s faster)
- MLDSA65-ES256 (-55): 6,291 B → 4,960 B (7,953.1 ms → 7,428.8 ms, 0.52s faster)
- ML-DSA-87 (-50): 6,676 B → 5,345 B (7,240.6 ms → 5,879.8 ms, 1.36s faster)
- MLDSA87-Ed448 (-59): 6,688 B → 5,357 B (7,281.9 ms → 6,870.0 ms, 0.41s faster)
- MLDSA87-ES384 (-56): 6,728 B → 5,397 B (9,136.6 ms → 8,514.6 ms, 0.62s faster)
- 100% Pass on Consolidated fmt: "mtc" (Authr-MakeCred-Resp-1 P-01..P-04, F-01) & Metadata-Stmt-1 (25 / 25): All 18 algorithms verified cleanly under P-04 (0.165s), confirming attStmt.pubKey === undefined, batchKeys lookup by serial, RFC 9162 Merkle inclusion proof reduction, HSS-LMS (alg: -46) subtree checkpoint signature verification against metadata.attestationRootCertificates, and ML-DSA-44 batch signature verification.
- Root Cause & Immediate Fix of the 4 Test-Harness TypeErrors: The only 4 test failures in Run 22 (P-05 in Authr-MakeCred-Resp-1.js, and P-2, P-3, F-6 in EntepriseAttestation.js) occurred in newly added test-harness code that referenced window.config.test.fidoauthenticator.getInfo.extensions synchronously (TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'extensions')) before inspecting the authenticator response. Replacing that synchronous property lookup with the conformance runner's async getGetInfoCached() helper in both test files and repackaging app.asar resolved all 4 harness errors.

0.0000.5 Root-Cause Analysis & Resolution of the 4 Test-Harness TypeErrors (getGetInfoCached())

  • Observed Symptom (L11307, L18648, L18828, L19421):
  • All 4 failures (P-05 in Authr-MakeCred-Resp-1.js, and P-2, P-3, F-6 in EntepriseAttestation.js) threw the identical JavaScript TypeError inside the test script before sending or inspecting the mtc-debug extension:
TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'extensions')
  • Root Cause:
  • Our initial test-harness patch checked window.config.test.fidoauthenticator.getInfo.extensions.includes('mtc-debug'). In the FIDO Conformance Testing Tool, window.config.test.fidoauthenticator does not expose a synchronous .getInfo property (window.config.test.fidoauthenticator.getInfo is undefined); instead, authenticatorGetInfo is retrieved asynchronously via getGetInfoCached().
  • Fix Applied & Repackaged (app.asar):
  • In Authr-MakeCred-Resp-1.js (P-05), wrapped the test body in return getGetInfoCached().then((getInfo) => { if (!getInfo.extensions || !getInfo.extensions.includes('mtc-debug')) { this.skip(); return; } ... }).
  • In EntepriseAttestation.js, cached supportsMtcDebug = Boolean(getInfo.extensions && getInfo.extensions.includes('mtc-debug')) inside the suite's before() hook (which already invokes getGetInfoCached()) and replaced the synchronous property lookup in P-2, P-3, F-2, F-3, and F-6 with supportsMtcDebug ? { 'mtc-debug': true } : undefined, then repackaged /Applications/FIDO Alliance - Certification Conformance Testing Tools (Patched).app/Contents/Resources/app.asar.

1. Detailed Analysis of Authr-ClientPin2-PinPolicy F-2 Test Logic

1.1 Specification vs. Implementation in Fidont

Per CTAP 2.1 / 2.2 / 2.3 Section 6.5.5.5 (Setting a new PIN):

  • The platform zero-pads newPin into a fixed 64-byte buffer (newPin must be at most 63 UTF-8 bytes so that at least one trailing 0x00 byte terminates the PIN inside the 64-byte plaintext block).
  • The authenticator decrypts newPinEnc using sharedSecret to obtain paddedPin:
  • If the decrypted paddedPin buffer length is not 64 bytes, the ciphertext parameter itself is malformed (CTAP1_ERR_INVALID_PARAMETER (0x02)).
  • If the decrypted paddedPin buffer is 64 bytes (PIN_PADDED_LENGTH), the authenticator strips trailing 0x00 bytes and checks the unpadded PIN length: if no 0x00 byte is present (pin.len() == 64 > 63) or if pin_codepoints < minPINLength, it returns CTAP2_ERR_PIN_POLICY_VIOLATION (0x37).

Fidont implements this in client_pin.rs:67-102:

fn decrypt_pin<E: Env>(
    shared_secret: &SharedSecret<E>,
    new_pin_enc: Vec<u8>,
) -> CtapResult<Secret<[u8]>> {
    let decrypted_pin = shared_secret.decrypt(&new_pin_enc)?;
    if decrypted_pin.len() != PIN_PADDED_LENGTH { // PIN_PADDED_LENGTH = 64
        return Err(Ctap2StatusCode::CTAP1_ERR_INVALID_PARAMETER); // 0x02 (2)
    }
    let len = decrypted_pin
        .iter()
        .position(|&c| c == 0)
        .unwrap_or(decrypted_pin.len());
    let mut result = Secret::new(len);
    result.copy_from_slice(&decrypted_pin[..len]);
    Ok(result)
}

1.2 How ClientPin1 vs. ClientPin2 Construct F-2 and F-3

  • Authr-ClientPin1-PinPolicy.js (F-2 & F-3) — Authr-ClientPin1-PinPolicy.js:112-166: Both F-2 and F-3 generate a 64-byte non-zero PIN inside a 64-byte encryptionBuffer (generateRandomClientPinBuffer(64)). Because decrypted_pin.len() == 64 with no 0x00 terminator, Fidont always returns CTAP2_ERR_PIN_POLICY_VIOLATION (0x37).
  • Authr-ClientPin2-PinPolicy.js (F-2 vs F-3) — Authr-ClientPin2-PinPolicy.js:109-159:
  • F-3 ("Try setting new pin, that is exactly 64 bytes, and check that Authenticator returns error CTAP2_ERR_PIN_POLICY_VIOLATION(0x37)") encrypts a 64-byte encryptionBuffer (16B IV + 64B CT = 80 bytes) and asserts CTAP2_ERR_PIN_POLICY_VIOLATION (0x37).
  • F-2 ("Try setting new pin, that is bigger than 63 bytes, and check that Authenticator returns an error") generates a random-length buffer generateRandomClientPinBuffer(generateSecureRandomInt(64, 128)) and passes it to encryptAES256CBC (cryptodep.js:394-405), which calls cipher.update(messageBuffer) without cipher.final(), truncating the plaintext to ⌊L / 16⌋ × 16 bytes:
  • When L ∈ [64, 79] (24.6% chance), newPinEnc has 64 ciphertext bytes (80B total with IV) → CTAP2_ERR_PIN_POLICY_VIOLATION (0x37).
  • When L ∈ [80, 128] (75.4% chance), newPinEnc has 80, 96, 112, or 128 ciphertext bytes (96..144B total with IV) → CTAP1_ERR_INVALID_PARAMETER (0x02) because decrypted_pin.len() != 64.
  • Updating Authr-ClientPin2-PinPolicy.js:130 to assert.notStrictEqual(ctap2Response.statusCode, CTAP_ERROR_CODES.CTAP1_ERR_SUCCESS) matches the F-2 specification ("check that Authenticator returns an error") and the CTAP 2.1 module.
